+============================================================================
+VUT-TESTRUN -- DISCUSSIONS
+============================================================================
+
+What is OPEN. Nothing settled belongs here; the settled design is
+README.txt. An entry is deleted the moment it is settled, and what it
+settled goes into the README as mechanics.
+
+    disc-N   a CHOICE is owed -- Frank-Rene rules, then it is built
+    todo-N   the LABOUR is owed -- the choice is made, the work is not
+
+============================================================================
+
+
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+disc-8  THE GREATER MAP  (parked -- Frank-Rene: "that for later")
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+How a test sits in the world above it:
+
+    a DIRECTORY LIST   ->  a DIRECTORY  ->  a TEST LIST  ->  a TEST
+                                                              -> choices
+
+README's bound says selecting, ordering and scheduling MANY tests is a
+higher layer. What is NOT yet said is the seam: what that layer hands down
+(a TestConfiguration per application, presumably produced by the facade),
+and what it needs back (per-test verdicts, aggregated).
+
+Two things already discussed hang on this and should be settled with it,
+not before:
+
+    the build's two callers -- per test, or once for a directory then
+      fanned out (parallel safety against test self-containment)
+    scope-wide defaults -- a directory-wide cap stated once. Resolved
+      OUTSIDE (disc-9), so invisible here, but whatever resolves it must
+      be told what the scope IS -- and the scope is this map
+
+
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+disc-9  THE CONFIGURATION FILE  (parked -- OUTSIDE this component)
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+Frank-Rene: a configuration FILE is to be designed, and then how its
+content translates into the configuration. Both are outside test_run
+(README 2.7: the aggregate is handed in complete).
+
+Recorded here only for the SEAM, which IS ours: the translation's target
+is 'TestConfiguration' + 'TestChoiceConfiguration' as README 3 states
+them. Two obligations follow on this side:
+
+    the required shape is stated exactly, so a translator can be written
+      against it without reading our code
+
+    every field is declared by the component that ENFORCES it (README 2.7,
+      aggregation), so a file format change never reaches past the
+      translator
+
+    one CHECK is owed there and cannot be done here: two source files with
+      the same STEM would share 'BUILD/<stem>' (README 3). Colliding stems
+      must be REFUSED where configurations are made -- this component sees
+      one test at a time and cannot notice
+
+Sequence: the file is designed AFTER the shape is settled, not with it.
+
+
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+todo-2  RE-ASSOCIATION: WHAT IS STILL OPEN  (compare)
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+The SHAPE is settled and stands in README 11.7: a realignment re-folds the
+BLOCK the edit fell into, the block is a chunk, compare names it, the IDE
+quotes the name back. What is left is not a choice of algorithm but two
+gaps between that shape and the code.
+
+(a) THE OUTER TEXT HAS NO BLOCKS.  'AssociationChunkPipe' flushes an outer
+    chunk only at '##!', '####' or EOF, so a nominal without regions is
+    ONE block and "re-fold the block" is "re-fold everything".
+
+        with regions:   [outer][##!pot][outer][##!tbl]   4 blocks
+        without:        [ .......................... ]   1 block
+
+    Whether to cut the outer text -- and on what boundary, a blank-line
+    run being the obvious candidate -- is OPEN. Note it is INTERNAL: the
+    interface speaks block ids either way, so the clients do not care.
+    It is also CHECKABLE rather than arguable: split the outer text, run
+    compare's own suite, and see whether any GOOD file moves. If none
+    does, the boundary is free; if one does, the split changed what the
+    edit search may match across, and that is the real cost, named.
+
+(b) STARTING THE FOLD AT A BLOCK NEEDS THE STATE BEFORE IT, AND TWO
+    PIECES OF THAT STATE CANNOT BE CARRIED YET.
+
+        the analogy db     ALREADY carried -- 'ChunkPair' holds the
+                           snapshot that holds after it
+        the flyweight      created and destroyed PER 'associate()' call,
+        registry           so a snapshot from one round is meaningless in
+                           the next: its interned ids belong to a dead
+                           registry. Either the merge SESSION is one
+                           long-lived run across every round, or the
+                           snapshot is made registry-independent.
+        the constraint     no history at all, and its liveness is a
+        space              one-way latch. Until it can be carried, a
+                           re-folded block can produce a different cell
+                           than the full fold would.
+
+    The registry question is SHARED WITH todo-16 -- the loop's own shape
+    decides it -- so it is ruled there, not here.
+
+CORRECTNESS NEEDS NO JUDGEMENT and no measurement: re-folding from block B
+must equal 'feed(subject, working nominal)'. An exact oracle.
+
+
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+todo-12  A MISSPELT TOLERANCE IS ACCEPTED SILENTLY  (compare)
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+'compare.Configuration' is slotted, so a misspelt field on IT is refused.
+'ConfigurationPatternFinder' is not:
+
+    c.pattern_finder.numeric_tolerence_ratio = 0.01   accepted
+    c.pattern_finder.numeric_tolerance_ratio          still 0
+
+The author believes a tolerance is in force; it is not. Under 'absent and
+empty never collapse' this is the worst shape: the test then passes or
+fails for a reason nobody wrote down, and no error is raised anywhere.
+
+It is COMPARE's to fix -- '__slots__' on the pattern finder, as
+'Configuration' already has -- but it is recorded here because it is this
+component's authors who would be bitten.
+
+OWED, in the compare component:
+    '__slots__' on 'ConfigurationPatternFinder', listing its fields
+    a test choice: a misspelt attribute RAISES rather than being kept
+    nothing here changes -- 'TestChoiceConfiguration' holds compare's
+    struct verbatim either way
+
+
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+todo-15  THE CHANNEL  (parked -- LOW-HANGING FRUIT after todo-16)
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+Both halves work and now agree on the wire:
+
+    protocol.lua   folds a real DOWN stream, reads the two sides from
+                   the cells                        (6 choices, lua5.4)
+    view.lua       shows them as two bound panes, commits the nominal
+                   pane's plain text                (4 choices, nvim -l)
+    RemoteDisplay  speaks the protocol over a pipe  (mock client, python)
+
+What is missing is the piece that makes them ONE thing: a running neovim
+on the other end of RemoteDisplay's pipe, folding as messages arrive and
+answering with an UP envelope when the author decides.
+
+PARKED, and Frank-Rene expects it to be EASY once the merge LOOP
+(todo-16) is done -- the hard part is the alignment service behind the
+channel, not the channel. One thing about it is SETTLED: the shape is
+
+    hwut.difftool -t nvim
+
+so HWUT LAUNCHES THE TOOL; the tool does not call HWUT. An IDE invoking
+the diff itself is a later step again, and does not constrain this one.
+
+What remains open is only the mechanism -- 'nvim --listen' on a socket,
+'jobstart' from within nvim, or 'nvim -l' reading the pipe -- and that is
+a choice to take when the difftool front end is written, not before.
+
+It is TESTABLE HERE when the time comes: neovim is installed, 'nvim -l'
+drives it headlessly, and the mock client already proved the pipe shape.
+
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+todo-16  THE MERGE LOOP DOES NOT LOOP
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+WHAT A MERGE IS. The author is shown the comparison and edits the nominal
+until it says what the test should from now on accept. Editing changes the
+ALIGNMENT -- which line stands beside which -- and the alignment is
+COMPARE's, never the editor's. So after an edit the author must be shown
+the comparison AGAIN, computed afresh. That "again", repeated until the
+author commits or abandons, is the loop.
+
+WHAT EXISTS TODAY. 'feed.merge_session' presents the comparison once, asks
+the driver once, and returns:
+
+    open  ->  present every DOWN item  ->  resolve  ->  close  ->  return
+
+'Resolution.intent' may be COMMIT, CANCEL or REALIGN. COMMIT and CANCEL
+are handled. REALIGN is declared in 'E_Intent' and acted upon by NOTHING:
+the value arrives and the function returns anyway. So an author sees the
+FIRST association and every edit after it is shown against a stale
+alignment.
+
+WHAT IT MUST BECOME.
+
+    open                                     ONCE, at the start
+      |
+      +--> present every DOWN item  <--------------------.
+      |                                                   |
+      +--> resolve  -> REALIGN (working nominal) ---------'
+      |             -> COMMIT  (final nominal) --.
+      |             -> CANCEL  ------------------+
+      |                                          |
+    close                                    ONCE, at the end
+      |
+      v
+    return (nominal_text, intent)
+
+The braces matter. 'open' and 'close' stay OUTSIDE the loop -- a driver
+whose connection IS the session (a pipe, a socket) has nothing left to
+answer on once it is closed, which is why 'resolve' already sits inside
+the open session today. Only present/resolve repeat.
+
+Each round is one fresh call to 'compare_feeder.feed(...)' with the SAME
+subject and the round's working nominal. That is cheap to arrange: this
+hub already holds both sides as TEXT and wraps them in 'io.StringIO' per
+call, so nothing has to be re-read from anywhere. The subject is FIXED
+throughout -- only the nominal evolves.
+
+BUILT, AWAITING ITS ORACLES:
+
+    'merge_session' loops. 'open' and 'close' sit outside; each round is
+    one fresh 'compare_feeder.feed(subject, working nominal)'.
+
+    TWO GUARDS, both ending the session as a CANCEL so the nominal is
+    left exactly as it was:
+
+        NO PROGRESS  a REALIGN with no artifact, or one byte-identical
+                     to the round before it. Catches the ordinary bug
+                     -- a driver echoing its input -- on the very next
+                     round, and never touches an author, since every
+                     real edit progresses.
+        THE CAP      'MERGE_ROUND_MAX' (1000, and a caller may name its
+                     own). The backstop for a driver that OSCILLATES
+                     (A, B, A, B ...) and so progresses for ever
+                     without ever deciding -- which no-progress alone
+                     cannot see.
+
+    ENDED, NOT RAISED. A hung driver must not take its caller down with
+    it, and the nominal is untouched either way. The cost is that a
+    driver bug reads as CANCEL, exactly like an author who changed
+    their mind; if that becomes a nuisance in practice, the cap is the
+    place to make it loud.
+
+    'RemoteDisplay.resolve' NO LONGER CLOSES the client's stdin. A
+    REALIGN is answered with a fresh DOWN on that same pipe, so closing
+    it after the first answer made the loop impossible. 'close()' closes
+    it, once, when the session is really over.
+
+    'mock/mock_ide.py' takes one intent PER ROUND
+    ('mock_ide.py realign commit') and keeps reading after answering, so
+    the loop is exercised without an editor. A 'realign' answer appends
+    a line to the nominal it was handed -- it must progress, or the hub
+    rightly ends the session. Single-round behaviour is unchanged, so
+    the 'rich_client' oracle still holds.
+
+    THREE NEW CHOICES in 'TEST/test-merge.py':
+        loop            open/close once; COMMIT through a REALIGN; the
+                        no-progress refusal; twenty progressing rounds
+                        not cut off
+        loop_realigns   the second DOWN is a DIFFERENT association --
+                        the point of the loop, not a side effect
+        loop_pipe       the same across a real process boundary, which
+                        is what pins the stdin change above
+
+    OWED: their GOOD files. They must be baselined on the real tree by
+    running the choices there -- an oracle is generated on real
+    infrastructure, never written by hand.
+
+STILL OWED (phase 2, protocol):
+
+    'block_id' on 'SectionBeginInst' and on 'Resolution' (README 11.7),
+    the regenerated 'SIGNATURE_UI_PROTOCOL.txt' that follows from it,
+    and the field carried through 'protocol.lua'. The hub records the id
+    and still re-feeds in full: nothing behaves differently yet, and
+    after it no client ever changes again.
+
+THE ONE RULING OWED HERE. Compare offers alignment through 'feed()', and
+one call to it is one RUN: it sets up its own internal state at the start
+and tears it down at the end. The question is how a session of N rounds
+maps onto runs:
+
+    ONE RUN PER ROUND        round 1: [ run ]   round 2: [ run ]   ...
+                             Simple. Each round is independent and exact.
+                             Nothing carries over, and nothing needs to.
+
+    ONE RUN PER SESSION      round 1: [ run ................... ] round N
+                             Only needed if a later round wants to REUSE
+                             what an earlier one computed.
+
+Per round is the obvious default and is all a full re-feed needs. It is
+recorded as a ruling only because of what it forecloses: compare's
+flyweight registry is created and destroyed WITH the call, so a state
+snapshot taken in round 1 means nothing in round 2 -- its interned
+identities belong to a registry that no longer exists. Block-scoped
+re-folding (todo-2) is exactly a later round reusing an earlier one's
+state, so if per-round is ruled, todo-2's remaining work becomes "make the
+snapshot registry-independent" rather than "keep one run alive".
+
+Neither answer blocks this entry: A FULL RE-FEED PER ROUND IS ALREADY A
+CORRECT REALIGNMENT, exact by construction. The loop therefore does not
+wait for block scoping. Block scoping waits for the loop -- and the full
+re-feed is the oracle it must equal.
+
+
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+todo-17  A SECOND IDE  (VS Code, Notepad++, whatever comes)
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+What the nvim work leaves for the next editor, measured:
+
+    feed.py               494 lines   REUSED WHOLE. The Python side is
+                                      editor-neutral: JSON lines out, one
+                                      UP envelope back.
+    down_fixture.json       --        REUSED WHOLE. The same oracle in a
+                                      neutral form, so a client in any
+                                      language is held against exactly
+                                      what the Lua client is held against.
+    protocol.lua          154 lines   RE-IMPLEMENTED. The protocol is
+                                      language-neutral; this file is not.
+                                      Fold DOWN, read cells, build UP.
+    view.lua              125 lines   REPLACED ENTIRELY. Editor API.
+
+So a second RICH client costs roughly 150 lines of protocol plus whatever
+its editor's own view needs -- and none of the Python side moves.
+
+THE CHEAP PATH FIRST. Any editor that can be launched on files already
+works through 'MergeToolDisplay', with no client at all:
+
+    code --wait --diff <subject> <nominal>
+    notepad++ -multiInst -nosession <subject> <nominal>
+
+That is tier 1, and it is what a new editor should get before anyone
+writes a plugin for it. A RICH client earns its cost only where the
+editor can render compare's OWN alignment -- which is the whole reason
+the tier exists, since an editor's own diff is a SECOND opinion beside
+the one the verdict came from.
+
+OWED, when a second client is wanted:
+    a conformance suite driven from 'down_fixture.json', so every client
+      answers the same questions
+    the four the Lua client answers already: fold, foreign signature,
+      unknown kind, truncated stream
+
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+todo-18  A BROWSER CLIENT  (the most universal RICH tier)
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+IT NEEDS NO LIBRARY, and that was measured rather than assumed: DOWN is
+already JSON, so a stdlib 'http.server' streaming Server-Sent Events
+delivers it, and two columns are plain DOM.
+
+    server   python stdlib, SSE          zero dependencies
+    render   two scroll-bound columns    plain DOM
+    edit     a textarea                  enough for a first client
+
+WHY IT IS THE BEST DISPLAY CLIENT, better than any editor: every cell
+already carries WHY it differs --
+
+    { "subject": "WRONG", "relation_id": "BAD_NOMINAL_DIFFERS",
+      "tolerance_id": "STRING", "nominal_ref_i": 0 }
+
+-- so a browser can colour per cell: this DIFFERED, this was tolerated
+as a number, this was matched by analogy. An editor's buffer API makes
+that awkward; a stylesheet makes it trivial. And the alignment shown is
+still COMPARE's, which is the whole point of the tier.
+
+WHERE IT IS WEAKER: editing. An author merging a large nominal wants an
+editor. So the natural split is
+
+    DifferenceDisplay (read-only)  -> the browser is the best client
+    Accept / merge    (editing)    -> an editor, or a browser with a
+                                      real editing component
+
+EDIT THE ROWS, NOT A TEXT BUFFER. The obvious move is to embed an editor
+(CodeMirror, Monaco, Ace all exist and are embeddable). It is the wrong
+one: a free-form editor owns its own wrapping, breaking and scrolling,
+and the nominal pane must stay ROW-ALIGNED with the subject, because the
+alignment is COMPARE's. The effort would go into fighting the editor
+back into alignment.
+
+Compare hands over ROWS, so the natural model is one editable element
+per row:
+
+    row 1   [ alpha  ]  [ alpha ]      read-only | contenteditable
+    row 2   [ WRONG  ]  [ beta  ]      alignment is STRUCTURAL: a cell
+    row 3   [ gamma  ]  [ gamma ]      IS its row and cannot drift
+
+No library, no wrapping problem, no scroll-binding to maintain, and
+per-cell colouring from 'relation_id' comes free -- which a text editor
+actively prevents.
+
+WHAT IS LOST: multi-line edits that split or join rows. That is real, and
+the loop already answers it: splitting a row CHANGES THE ALIGNMENT, which
+is compare's to decide. Edit the cells, send REALIGN (todo-16), let
+compare produce the new rows.
+
+A LIBRARY EARNS ITS PLACE only if the nominal is large, or wants syntax
+highlighting, or undo across rows -- none of which is true of a typical
+expected-output file. Build the row-cell version first.
+
+IF ONE IS EVER USED, one law carries over unchanged: its OWN diff view
+must stay off. Monaco ships one, and using it would put a SECOND
+alignment beside the one the verdict came from -- the same reason
+'test-view.lua' asserts neovim's diff is not engaged. Check what those
+libraries currently offer before choosing; this note should not be
+trusted for their present state.
+
+NOTEPAD++ specifically: plugins are C++ and there is no scripting host in
+the box, so tier 1 ('MergeToolDisplay' launching it on two files) is
+likely the permanent answer there rather than a RICH client.
+
+OWED, when wanted:
+    reuse 'down_fixture.json' as the conformance oracle (todo-17)
+    the UP envelope over the same channel -- a POST, or a websocket if
+      the merge loop (todo-16) wants full duplex
+
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+todo-19  THE NUMERIC TOLERANCE IS UNSIGNED AND ASYMMETRIC  (compare)
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+PHILOSOPHY 1.1 demands that Eq(D(b0), D(b), T) NOT be a function of b0 --
+the verdict must not depend on which good run happened to be accepted.
+Checking our own compare against that found two defects, measured:
+
+    'compare/engine/input/line_element.py', LineElementNumber
+
+        self.epsilon = self.number * numeric_tolerance_ratio
+
+    NO abs(). For a NEGATIVE number the epsilon is negative, and
+
+        error = max(|s - n| - epsilon, 0)
+
+    becomes |s - n| + |epsilon|. The tolerance works BACKWARDS: a 1%
+    tolerance makes negative numbers HARDER to match than no tolerance.
+
+        epsilon( 100.0) =  1.0    d( 100.0,  100.5) = 0.0       tolerated
+        epsilon(-100.0) = -1.0    d(-100.0, -100.5) = 0.014925  NOT
+
+    ASYMMETRIC. The epsilon comes from 'self' alone, so Eq(s, n) and
+    Eq(n, s) use different bands:
+
+        d(-100.0, -100.5) = 0.014925
+        d(-100.5, -100.0) = 0.014975
+
+    Which is precisely the failure PHILOSOPHY 1.1 names: accept 0.815 and
+    the band sits there; accept 0.816 and it sits elsewhere; a value near
+    the edge is admissible or not depending on WHICH RUN WAS ACCEPTED.
+
+OWED, in compare:
+    epsilon from the magnitude, symmetrically -- 'max(abs(s), abs(n)) *
+      ratio' at comparison time, not stored per element
+    test choices: a negative pair inside the band is TOLERATED; d(x,y)
+      equals d(y,x) for signed pairs across the boundary
+    then state the MARGIN CONDITION in compare's own manual:
+      diameter(D(GOOD)) <= T < distance(D(GOOD), D(BAD))
+
+NOTHING IS OWED HERE. 'TestChoiceConfiguration' holds compare's struct
+verbatim; the defect and its fix are entirely compare's.
+============================================================================
+
+todo-20  DOWN CARRIES THE MEASUREMENT, NOT JUST THE VERDICT  (compare feeder)
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+The TUI tier (interaction/tui.py) rendered every element and region kind, and the
+exercise drew the line sharply between meta DOWN CARRIES and meta it
+does not. The client law held throughout: what DOWN lacks is compare's
+to emit, never the client's to compute -- a client re-deriving a
+deviation would re-implement tolerance semantics and drift.
+
+CARRIED TODAY, and rendered: the setup (ConfigInst), section boundaries
+(SectionBeginInst: title, chunk_type), per-cell relation + tolerance
+kind, the line pair's cost, and the analogy provenance
+(analogy_origin_line_number_pair -- first binding site, and the
+conflicting binding's site after the feeder's bake fix).
+
+DELIVERED (the protocol hash bumped once for both; the four signature
+agreement points and the hash-bearing GOODs moved with it):
+
+    numeric deviation     'LinePairInst.numeric_list': NumericMeta
+                          (cell_i, subject, nominal, deviation, limit)
+                          -- computed IN THE FEEDER via the engine's own
+                          'LineElementNumber' (number, epsilon), so the
+                          arithmetic cannot drift from the verdict's.
+                          The limit is the NOMINAL's epsilon. The TUI
+                          renders: 'off by 5 against a limit of 0.04
+                          (125.0x over)' / 'off by 0.001, within the
+                          limit of 0.03141'. (todo-19's fix will show
+                          here the day the epsilon becomes symmetric.)
+    matched pattern       'LinePairInst.pattern_list': PatternMeta
+                          (cell_i, side, pattern) -- re-matched against
+                          the SAME configured pattern list the reading
+                          lexed with. The TUI renders: "pattern:
+                          matches 'v[0-9.]+-build[0-9]+'".
+
+STILL OWED, in compare (deeper than the feeder can reach):
+
+    point-cloud distance  same shape as the numeric: measured distance
+                          vs 'limit' -- the measure lives in the
+                          region's association, not on the line pair
+    region parameters     SectionBeginInst says POTPOURRI but not
+                          'max_comparisons=64': the chunks CONSUME their
+                          resolved params at construction (unpacked into
+                          attributes, dict discarded) -- retaining them
+                          means touching the region constructors, so it
+                          waits for its own round
+    constraint verdicts   a binding is shown entering the space; the
+                          EXPRESSIONS it was checked against and their
+                          verdicts are not carried
+    transpose partner     BAD_TRANSPOSE says out-of-order; the partner
+                          line it transposed WITH would let a display
+                          draw the crossing
+
+The TUI's notes ('^ ...') are the display slot all of these land in;
+the html feeder's tooltips are the same slot. No renderer changes
+shape when these arrive -- one more note per line, that is all.
+============================================================================
+
+todo-21  THE EXPLAIN SEAM -- every silent failure names itself
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+Earned by a real evening: HWUT 1.0 answered a broken nvim interview with
+'[NO INFO]' and an empty string. The empty string collapsed five distinct
+events -- never spawned / died / wrong interpreter / said nothing / said
+it ON THE OTHER CHANNEL -- and the decisive evidence (stderr, which held
+the entire answer; nvim -l routes print() there) was collected and
+thrown away. Debugging meant editing the framework to add prints.
+
+2.0 already HOLDS every missing fact by construction: ProcsitterResult
+(argv, containment, exit, stderr tail, timings) rides in
+Supply.record_list and ProvisionRecord.records even in failure; stderr
+is a SUBJECT, so the wrong-channel bug is visible in the data. What is
+missing is PRESENTATION: the records mostly die unread.
+
+DELIVERED (diagnosis.py + TEST/test-diagnosis.py): explain() renders
+REQUEST (argv from the one source), the resolution chain, every
+record's containment/exit/stderr-tail, the report token, the channels
+with the wrong-channel hint; DiagnosisObserver hangs it on the seam
+(12). STILL OWED beyond the list below: the resolution chain CARRIED
+IN THE RECORD (today it is recomputed at render time -- honest for a
+live diagnosis, not for an archived one), and session-level wiring (a
+request flag that installs the observer).
+
+OWED:
+
+    the EXPLAIN observer   one switch that renders, per supervised
+                           call: argv; the RESOLUTION CHAIN (shutil
+                           .which + symlink chase -- 'nvim ->
+                           /usr/local/bin/nvim -> /opt/...'); cwd;
+                           exit; containment token; duration; per
+                           channel the byte count and first line.
+                           An OBSERVER on the existing seam (12) --
+                           no operation changes.
+    resolution reporting   resolve interpreters at argv construction
+                           via shutil.which (the shell's semantics --
+                           NEVER whereis), and carry the resolved
+                           chain into the record so EXPLAIN can show
+                           it. INTERPRETER_NOT_FOUND already exists;
+                           now it can say what was searched.
+    the wrong-channel hint 'stdout 0 bytes, stderr N>0' is a nameable
+                           pattern: one display-side note in the
+                           explain rendering ('output on the wrong
+                           channel?'). A HINT, never a verdict --
+                           provision judges nothing.
+
+THREE LAWS, distilled from the archaeology, for any runner code here:
+    (1) an empty answer is never reported empty -- it is reported WITH
+        exit code, containment, and the other channel's tail;
+    (2) a diagnostic path uses THE SAME call convention as the path it
+        diagnoses (1.0's whereis fallback broke this: string vs list,
+        and manufactured the red herring);
+    (3) children inherit the environment; resolution follows PATH.
+============================================================================
+
+todo-22  COVERAGE IN THE PACK  ('hwut.report -c')
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+The flag is ACCEPTED and RESERVED today: 'hwut.report x.py c -c' answers
+with a COVERAGE section that says the gathering is owed. Reserved, and
+honest about it -- a flag that silently did nothing would be the very
+'[NO INFO]' failure todo-21 was earned from.
+
+WHAT IS OWED. Coverage is a MEASUREMENT of a run, so it belongs where
+every other measurement of a run belongs: to PROVISION, as a sidecar
+beside the record, written by the store -- never re-derived by a
+service. The pack then only READS it, as it reads '.times'.
+
+    the source           the corpus already runs its tests under
+                         'coverage run --omit ./*.py -a *.py'
+                         (hwut-info.dat), leaving '.coverage' in the
+                         TEST directory -- a datum nobody currently
+                         collects
+    the sidecar          'store.write_coverage(test, choice, subject?)'
+                         beside 'write_timing': the per-file line hit
+                         counts of THIS test/choice, as data (JSON),
+                         never a rendered report -- the pack renders,
+                         the store stores
+    who gathers          an option of the RUN, like 'record_timing':
+                         'StoreConfig.record_coverage'. A coverage run
+                         is a DIFFERENT supervised call (the tool wraps
+                         the application), so it is the execute stage's
+                         argv that changes -- one place, and the
+                         'E_TestRunResult' vocabulary needs no new
+                         token: a coverage tool that fails is a build/
+                         launch failure like any other
+    the rendering        in the pack: per source file, percentage and
+                         the UNCOVERED line ranges -- the ranges are
+                         what a conversation about a test needs ('these
+                         branches were never taken'), a percentage
+                         alone says nothing actionable
+    the discipline       MACHINE-FREE like the rest of the pack: line
+                         numbers and counts, never absolute paths, never
+                         timestamps
+
+OPEN QUESTION, for when it is built: coverage of WHAT -- the test
+application only, or the units it exercises? The second is what a
+tester actually wants ('did this test reach the code it claims to
+test'), and it needs the '--omit'/'--include' set to come from the
+test's own configuration, not from a global convention.
+============================================================================
+
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+todo-23  PROXY PROVISION -- what remains open
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+BUILT: the seam (provision/provider.py, one interface per role, refused
+at the door, planner-declared 'kind', cadence in the delivery) and THE
+MULTI-EXECUTOR (provision/multi_execute.py): one supervised '--interactive'
+call serves many choices through per-choice I_ExecuteProviders; tickets
+by token, sinks as relative transport under '.hwut-session/', a non-zero
+status delivers with report OK, 'fail' delivers nothing and names it.
+RULED: the capability is a CONFIGURATION key, 'interactive' -- registered,
+never probed. RULED, the general scheme: 'I_Provider' roots the family;
+'I_ProxyProvider(I_Provider)' is a provider handed out by an
+'I_MultiProvider' (the holder of one shared in-parallel means); the
+ORCHESTRATOR decides -- queue a plain provider, or generate a
+multi-provider and queue its proxies. MultiExecute/ChoiceExecute are the
+first concrete pair; a multi-builder and a configuration-management
+acquire stand on the same three interfaces.
+
+Still open, each awaiting its ruling:
+
+    (the attribution: RULED and BUILT. The record of the process that
+     produced a result is PART of that result, in every mode -- so every
+     choice's 'record_list' carries the session's ProcsitterResult, the
+     same one for each. It exists only after 'close()', hence the session
+     PRODUCES FIRST and is read afterwards; the sinks hold the products
+     meanwhile. Rejected: an empty carriage (silence about the producer);
+     a per-choice invented record (nothing to invent it from).)
+    file subjects        the current law: a session delivers the two
+                         CHANNEL subjects only; choices of one test share
+                         one 'OUT/', so file subjects would cross-pollute.
+                         A test with file subjects takes the classic
+                         one-call run -- until ruled otherwise
+    isolation            sequential interactive mode shares one process
+                         across choices (hwut_runner README, RESET
+                         DISCIPLINE); which suites accept that bargain is
+                         the orchestrator's dial, not this component's
+                         default
+    (one lock site: BUILT -- 'run_test_held', entered by the holder;
+     the home: DECIDED, vut/engine/orchestrator/ -- worker's placement,
+     standing until vetoed; see that component's RATIONALE D-1/D-2.)
+
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+(disc-1  THE RESULT STORE: RULED AND BUILT, and it left this component.)
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+ONE base, 'GOOD/result_db.json', JSON, REPLACES the footprint file; the
+footprint face, FOOTPRINT_FILE_NAME and '_write_footprint' are gone. The
+base, THE NAMING, the reproducible configurations and the divergence
+verdicts belong to the BOOKKEEPER (vut/engine/orchestrator/bookkeeper/);
+its RATIONALE D-1..D-6 holds the rulings, and the attribution record has
+its first durable home there. Here, the Store keeps the ARTIFACTS and the
+LOCK, is constructed OVER a Bookkeeper, and asks it for every path: the
+Bookkeeper answers WHERE, this component decides WHEN. The Bookkeeper is
+MADE ABOVE and handed to every mode.
+
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+todo-23  README DEEP-SECTION POLISH AFTER THE SURGERY
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+The surgery (test_run -> operations; provision pruned to
+execute -> canonicalise; building to the plan's BUILD nodes with
+'build_action.py'; loading to 'consume/loaded.py'; the store to the
+Bookkeeper's 'stream_store.py'; the accept ledger; the freshness
+sidecar) is IN. The README's head and every false statement were
+re-cut with it; the deep sections (3-7) still carry the OLD prose
+rhythm around the corrected facts and are owed one editorial pass.
+Until then: where a deep section and PART I disagree, PART I is the
+truth.
+
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+todo-24  THE STALENESS LAW  (s-4b, second half)
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+Every stored candidate now carries a freshness sidecar
+('<candidate>.when': instant, and the sha256 CONTENT digest of the
+test source). The LAW over it -- refuse, warn, or stay silent when an
+accept or diff consumes a stored subject whose source digest no longer
+matches -- is NOT ruled. Nothing depends on the ruling; history is
+being recorded either way.
+
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+todo-25  THE FACE OF THE SECOND QUESTION
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+S-1 is built beneath: the three states, the refusal, the mark. What is
+NOT built is the FACE -- 'hwut.accept' does not exist yet, so
+'--stderr-accept' and '--stderr-ignore' have nowhere to be typed. Two
+things fall to it:
+
+    - the three flags ('--stderr-nominal', '--stderr-ignored',
+      '--stderr-forbidden'), carried into 'Request.stderr' as an
+      E_StderrNote
+    - the INTERACTIVE form: where no flag stands and a terminal is
+      present, ASK -- the first answer accepts stdout, the second
+      writes the note (Frank-Rene's case 2)
+
+Until then, the decision travels only through 'Request.stderr' in
+code, and the refusal is what a tree-wide run reports.
+
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+todo-26  RENOVATE AND THE STDERR SURVEY
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+'hwut.renovate' should report which tests would newly refuse under S-1
+-- those that write on stderr with no nominal and no mark -- so a tree
+can be surveyed before the law is met test by test.
